\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Appendix B
Preliminaries

For our proofs, it will be useful to have some familiarity with quantum state designs and tensor-network diagrams.
A more detailed review can be found in Ref.
[
83
]
.

B.1
Quantum state designs

A
Haar-random state

|\psi\rangle
in the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
is a pure state drawn randomly according to the Haar measure
\mu_{\rm H}
on quantum states.
For integer
t\geq 1
, the expectation of
(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|)^{\otimes t}
with respect to the Haar measure reads

\displaystyle\underset{|\psi\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm H}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|)^{\otimes t}}}\right]=\frac{1}{\kappa_{t}}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(t)},\quad\kappa_{t}=\binom{t+d-1}{t},

(64)

where
\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(t)}
is the projector onto the symmetric subspace of
\mathcal{H}^{\otimes t}
,
and
\kappa_{t}
is the dimension of the symmetric subspace.

An ensemble
\mathcal{E}
of pure states in
\mathcal{H}
is said to be a state
t
-design if and only of
its
t
th moment agrees with that of the Haar distribution, i.e.,

\displaystyle\underset{|\psi\rangle\sim\mathcal{E}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|)^{\otimes t}}}\right]=\underset{|\psi\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm H}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|)^{\otimes t}}}\right]=\frac{1}{\kappa_{t}}\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(t)}.

(65)

If
\mathcal{E}
is a state
t
-design, then it is also a state
(t-1)
-design by definition.

Let
\mathrm{S}_{t}
be the permutation group of order
t
.
For any element
\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t}
, we define its corresponding permutation operator
to be the unitary operator
T_{\pi}
on
\mathcal{H}^{\otimes t}
that satisfies

\displaystyle T_{\pi}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{1}}}\right\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{t}}}\right\rangle}}\right)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{\pi^{-1}(1)}}}\right\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{\pi^{-1}(t)}}}\right\rangle

(66)

for
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{1}}}\right\rangle,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{2}}}\right\rangle,\ldots,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\varphi_{t}}}\right\rangle\in\mathcal{H}
.
For example, for
\pi=(1,2)\in\mathrm{S}_{2}
, the corresponding permutation operator
T_{(1,2)}
is the swap operator.
An important property of the projector
\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(t)}
is that it can be decomposed into a sum of permutation operators:

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(t)}=\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{t}}T_{\pi}.

(67)

A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
on the space
\mathcal{H}
is represented as a box with open legs:

where the top leg stands for row index of

are represented as

(72)

In tensor-network diagrams, permutation operators are represented as changing the position of legs.
For example, the swap operator is represented as exchanging two legs,
and cyclic permutation operators are represented as sequentially moving each leg to its neighboring position:

\displaystyle T_{(1,2)}=\ \hbox to24.3pt{\vbox to34.54pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 12.1501pt\lower-17.27182pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}

A particularly important diagram is that of the unnormalized maximally entangled state:

(74)

from which we can easily deduce that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|\Phi\rangle\langle\Phi|}}\right)^{\top_{2}}=T_{(1,2)}
.

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
1

The expectation of
\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}
(over both random state preparation and measurement outcomes) is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}\big]=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{K}(\phi_{j}\otimes\psi_{i})\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{\psi}=\psi_{i},\hat{\phi}=\phi_{j}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{K}(\phi_{j}\otimes\psi_{i})\frac{1}{L}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[A_{\phi_{j}}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\Bigg(\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\phi_{j}\Tr\!\Big[\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}\Big]\Bigg)\otimes\psi_{i}.

(75)

According to Lemma
5
below and the fact that
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
forms a state
(s+1)
-design (see Sec.
III.1
), the term in the parentheses in the last line can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\phi_{j}\Tr\!\Big[\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}\Big]=\frac{I+sU\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}{d+s}.

(76)

So we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}\big]=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{I+sU\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}{d+s}}}\right)\otimes\psi_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{I}{d+s}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\psi_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{s}{d+s}(U\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}}}\right)(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{I}{d+s}\otimes\frac{I}{\kappa_{1}}+\frac{s}{d+s}(U\otimes I)\frac{\Pi_{\mathrm{sym}}^{(2)}}{\kappa_{2}}(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\frac{I\otimes I}{\kappa_{1}(d+s)}+\frac{s}{2\kappa_{2}(d+s)}\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{S}_{2}}(U\otimes I)T_{\pi}(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)

\displaystyle=\frac{(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)+s(U\otimes I)T_{(1,2)}(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)}{d(d+1)(d+s)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\frac{(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)+s(U\otimes U^{{\dagger}})T_{(1,2)}}{d(d+1)(d+s)},

(77)

which confirms Lemma
1
in the main text.
Here
(a)
follows from Eq. (
4
) in the main text and the fact that
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
is a state 2-design (see Sec.
III.1
),

(b)
follows from Eq. (
67
), and
(c)
holds because
T_{(1,2)}(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)=(I\otimes U^{{\dagger}})T_{(1,2)}
.

In this proof, we use at most the
(s+1)
-design propertity of the ensemble
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
and the 2-design property of the ensemble
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
.
The
(s+2)
-design propertity of
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
and the
4
-design propertity of
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
will be used later in the proof of Lemma
6
.

The following auxiliary lemma follows directly from Lemma 13 in Ref.
[
11
]
.

Lemma 5
.

Suppose integer
s\geq 1
, pure state
|\varphi\rangle\in\mathcal{H}
, and
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
forms a state
(s+1)
-design on
\mathcal{H}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j}\phi_{j}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}\varphi^{\otimes s}}}\right)=\frac{I+s\varphi}{d+s}.

(78)

Appendix D
Auxiliary lemmas

To establish our results in the main text, here we prepare several auxiliary lemmas.

Recall that our algorithm
\mathcal{A}_{\text{learn}}
working in the learning phase outputs a classical description of the prepared state
\hat{\psi}
and the measurement outcome
\hat{\phi}
in each round (see Sec.
III.1
).
In the main text, we have clarified the expectation of their tensor product
\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}
in Lemma
1
.
Here, we calculate the expectation of
\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}
, which is useful for proving Lemma
2
in the main text (see Appendix
F
).

Lemma 6
.

The expectation of
\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}

(over both random state preparation and measurement outcomes) is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\Delta_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\Delta_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\Delta_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\Delta_{4}}}\right),

(79)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)},

(80)

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}

\displaystyle:=(I\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I_{1}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}}\right)_{2,3,4}}}\right](I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right),

(81)

\displaystyle\Delta_{3}

\displaystyle:=(U\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I_{2}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}}\right)_{1,3,4}}}\right](U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right),

(82)

\displaystyle\Delta_{4}

\displaystyle:=(U\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\,\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(4)}\,(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I),

(83)

I_{i}
denotes the identity operator acting on the
i
th system, and

(\cdot)_{i,j,k}
denotes the operator acting on the
i
th,
j
th, and
k
th systems.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{j}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{\psi}=\psi_{i},\hat{\phi}=\phi_{j}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{j}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\frac{1}{L}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[A_{\phi_{j}}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\Bigg(\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\phi_{j}^{\otimes 2}\Tr\!\Big[\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}\Big]\Bigg)\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}.

(84)

According to Lemma
7
below and the fact that
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
forms a state
(s+2)
-design (see Sec.
III.1
),

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\phi_{j}^{\otimes 2}\Tr\!\Big[\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes s}\Big]=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I+sU\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}-\frac{s(s+1)}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}.

(85)

So we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I+sU\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}-\frac{s(s+1)}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I^{\otimes 2}+sI\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)+s\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)\otimes I+\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}.

(86)

Since
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
is a state 4-design (see Sec.
III.1
), we can apply Eq. (
4
) in the main text to calculate the sum in Eq. (
86
) term-by-term.
The first term is calculated as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I^{\otimes 2}\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}=\frac{\Delta_{1}}{\kappa_{2}},

(87)

where we have used the 2-design property of
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
.
The second term is calculated as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[sI\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=\frac{s}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}(I\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 3}}}\right)(I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)

\displaystyle=s(I\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\bigg(I\otimes\frac{\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}{\kappa_{3}}\bigg)(I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)=\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\Delta_{2},

(88)

where we have used the 3-design property of
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
.
By a similar reasoning, the third term is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[s\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)\otimes I}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}=\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\Delta_{3},

(89)

The last term is calculated as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}\,\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right]\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}(U\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\psi_{i}^{\otimes 4}}}\right)(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\frac{s(s-1)}{2}(U\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\,\frac{\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(4)}}{\kappa_{4}}\,(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)=\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\Delta_{4},

(90)

where
(a)
holds because
(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes 2}\,\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}=(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}})^{\otimes 2}
, and
(b)

holds because
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
is a 4-design.
Equations (
86
)–(
90
) together confirm Lemma
6
.
∎

Note that, in the above proof, the 4-design property of
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
is only utilized in the case of
s\geq 2
.
When
s=1
, the last term
\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U\psi_{i}U^{{\dagger}}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}
in the bracket of Eq. (
86
) vanishes and thus the 3-design property of
\{|\psi_{i}\rangle\}_{i=1}^{L}
is sufficient to prove Lemma
6
.

The following auxiliary lemma follows directly from Lemma 14 in Ref.
[
11
]
.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose integer
s\geq 1
, pure state
|\varphi\rangle\in\mathcal{H}
, and
\{|\phi_{j}\rangle\}_{j=1}^{K}
forms a state
(s+2)
-design on
\mathcal{H}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{s}}{K}\sum_{j}\phi_{j}^{\otimes 2}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{j}^{\otimes s}\varphi^{\otimes s}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I+s\varphi}}\right)^{\otimes 2}-\frac{s(s+1)}{2}\varphi^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}.

(91)

Lemma 8

(Hölder’s inequality)
.

Suppose
A,B
are operators on
\mathcal{H}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(AB}}\right)}}\right|\leq\|A\|_{1}\|B\|,\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(AB}}\right)}}\right|\leq\|A\|_{2}\|B\|_{2}.

(92)

Lemma 9
.

Suppose
U
is a unitary operator on
\mathcal{H}
,
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
, and
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.
Let
O_{U}:=U^{{\dagger}}OU
and
\wp:=\Tr(\rho^{2})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{dB}.

(93)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\min\{\sqrt{B\wp},1\},

(94)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\min\{\sqrt{B\wp},1\},

(95)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho^{2})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\wp,

(96)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho^{2})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\wp,

(97)

\displaystyle\big|\!\Tr(O_{U}\rho\,O_{U}\rho)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\wp.

(98)

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Equation (
93
) can be derived as

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|I}}\right\|_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O}}\right\|_{2}=\sqrt{d\Tr(O^{2})}\leq\sqrt{dB},

(99)

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
8
, and the second inequality holds because
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.

Similarly, Eq. (
94
) can be derived as

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O_{U}}}\right\|_{2}\|\rho\|_{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O}}\right\|_{2}\|\rho\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Tr(O^{2})\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{2}}}\right)}\leq\sqrt{B\wp},

(100)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O_{U}}}\right\|\|\rho\|_{1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O}}\right\|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho}}\right)\leq 1,

(101)

given that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O}}\right\|\leq 1
when
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.

Equation (
95
) follows from Eq. (
94
) and the fact that
O^{2}\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
when
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.

Equation (
96
) can be derived as

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho^{2})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O_{U}}}\right\|\|\rho^{2}\|_{1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O}}\right\|\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{2}}}\right)\leq\wp.

(102)

Equation (
97
) follows from Eq. (
96
) and the fact that
O^{2}\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
when
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.

Equation (
98
) can be derived as

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr(O_{U}\rho O_{U}\rho)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|O_{U}\rho}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\Tr[(O_{U}\rho)^{\dagger}(O_{U}\rho)]

\displaystyle=\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho^{2})\leq\wp,

(103)

where the last inequality follows from Eq. (
97
).
∎

Appendix E
Predicting
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
by averaging
\Tr[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}]

Recall that our protocol for CSEU
outputs the classical shadow data
\mathrm{UShadow}(\mathcal{U},m)=\{\hat{X}_{1},X_{2},\dots,\hat{X}_{m}\}
in the learning phase,
which contains
m
independent classical snapshots.
Given any quantum state
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
and observable
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
in the prediction phase,
as mentioned in Sec.
III.2
of the main text,
a simple way to predict
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
is
to calculate
\Tr[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}]
and take their average:

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{m}(O,\rho):=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}}}\right].

(104)

In this appendix we shall show that, with this method,
the number of queries required to estimate
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
within additive error
\epsilon

scales as
\mathcal{O}(d^{2}B/\epsilon^{2})
even when collective measurements on multiple systems are used. If only single-copy measurements are accessible,
then the query complexity further increases, reaching up to
\mathcal{O}(d^{3}B/\epsilon^{2})
.

The fluctuations of
\tilde{Z}_{m}(O,\rho)
around its expectation
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
are controlled by the
variance. By Lemma
10
below, we have

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\tilde{Z}_{m}(O,\rho)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\,\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)dB}}\right].

(105)

where the bound can be saturated when
\rho
is a pure state and
O=\sqrt{B/d}\,I\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.

By Chebyshev’s inequality, in order to suppress the additive error of
\tilde{Z}_{m}(O,\rho)
to
\epsilon
with
some constant success probability
1-\delta
, it suffices to choose

m
such that
\Var\!\big[\tilde{Z}_{m}(O,\rho)\big]\leq\delta\epsilon^{2}
; that is, to choose

\displaystyle m\geq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)dB\epsilon^{-2}

(106)

for some constant
C>0
.
So the total number of required queries reads

\displaystyle s\cdot m=C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)\frac{sdB}{\epsilon^{2}}.

(107)

In the special case
s=1
, i.e., only single-copy measurements are used, we have
s\cdot m=\mathcal{O}(d^{3}B/\epsilon^{2})
.
This query complexity agrees with that of the protocol for CSEC developed in Ref.
[
21
]
,
which is ancilla-free and does not use quantum memory (see Sec.
VI
in the main text).
If collective measurements on multiple systems are accessible, the query number can be further reduced by increasing
s
.
When
s=\Theta(d)
, it can be reduced to the greatest extent, in which case the query complexity reads
s\cdot m=\mathcal{O}(d^{2}B/\epsilon^{2})
.

In contrast to the above method, our method for estimating
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
in the main text is based on the quadratic estimator
d^{-1}\Tr[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}]
instead of
\Tr[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}]
.
As shown in Table
3
below,
in both single-copy and collective measurement scenarios, the quadratic estimator demonstrates strictly better query efficiency.

Table 3:

The query complexity of two different methods for estimating
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)
.

\begin{array}[]{c|cc}\hline\cr\hline\cr\mbox{Method}&\text{Single-copy measurements with $s=1$\ }&\text{\ Collective measurements with $s=\Theta(d)$}\\[2.58334pt]
\hline\cr\\[-12.05553pt]
\text{Averaging $\Tr\!\big[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}\big]$}&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{3}B\epsilon^{-2}}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}B\epsilon^{-2}}}\right)\\[4.73611pt]
\hline\cr\\[-10.33327pt]
\text{Averaging $d^{-1}\Tr\!\big[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\big]$}&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\,\epsilon^{-2}+d^{2}\sqrt{B}\epsilon^{-1}}}\right)\ \text{[see Eq.~{(\ref{eq:singleUB})}]}&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\,\big(\epsilon^{-2}+\sqrt{B}\epsilon^{-1}\big)}}\right)\ \text{[see Eq.~{(\ref{eq:jointUB})}]}\\[6.45831pt]
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Lemma 10
.

Suppose
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
and
O\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
. Then

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right)}}\right]\leq\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)dB}}\right],

(108)

where the inequality can be saturated when
\rho
is a pure state and
O=\sqrt{d^{-1}B}\,I
.

Note that when the observable
O
is proportional to the identity, i.e.,
O=cI
, the single-shot estimator

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(cI\otimes\rho^{\top})\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\top})-(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)}{s}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c\,d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}\Tr(\rho\hat{\psi})-\frac{c\,d(d+1+s)}{s}

(109)

is not a constant, and has fluctuation due to the randomness of
\hat{\psi}
.
Hence, the variance of
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right]
does not vanish in this case.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Using the definition of
\hat{X}
in Eq. (
9
) of the main text, we can expand the variance as

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\top})}}\right]-\frac{d+1+s}{s}\Tr(O\otimes\rho^{\top})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho)(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi})}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Tr[(O\otimes\rho)(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi})]}}\right)^{2}}}\right]-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr((O\otimes\rho)(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}))}}\right]}}\right)^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\Tr\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Big].

(110)

According to Lemma
6
, the trace term in the last line of Eq. (
110
) can be expanded as

\displaystyle\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Big]=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\Delta_{1}}{\kappa_{2}}+\frac{s\Delta_{2}}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s\Delta_{3}}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s(s-1)\Delta_{4}}{2\kappa_{4}}}}\right)}}\right].

(111)

Next, we calculate
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{j}}}\right]
for
j=1,2,3,4
, respectively.
First,

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{1}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho^{\otimes 2}\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)=\frac{1}{4}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[O^{\otimes 2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I+T_{(12)}}}\right)}}\right]\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\rho^{\otimes 2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I+T_{(12)}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr(O^{2})+(\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right))^{2}}}\right]\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr(\rho^{2})+1}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Bd}}\right),

(112)

where the last equality follows from Lemma
9
in Appendix
D
.
Second,
\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{2}}}\right]
can be calculated via tensor-network diagrams:

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)(I\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}}\right)(I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\ \hbox to120.3pt{\vbox to154.05pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-25.2074pt\lower-133.92787pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#CCFFCC} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} {}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 188.98 -90.55 L 188.98 -182.16}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{-1.0}{1.0}{0.0}{136.57332pt}{-128.96791pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.0 -1.0 1.0 0.0 188.98 -178.45)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.88 3.32 C 2.35 1.33 1.18 0.39 0 0 C 1.18 -0.39 2.35 -1.33 2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.0}{-1.0}{1.0}{0.0}{136.57332pt}{-131.44789pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(0.0 -1.0 1.0 0.0 188.98 -181.88)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.88 3.32 C 2.35 1.33 1.18 0.39 0 0 C 1.18 -0.39 2.35 -1.33 2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right)+\mathrm{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O}}\right)\Tr(\rho^{2})+2\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}})+2\Tr(OU\rho^{2}U^{{\dagger}})}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{12}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr(O^{2})+\Tr(O^{2})\Tr(\rho^{2})+2\Tr(O^{2}U\rho U^{{\dagger}})+2\Tr(O^{2}U\rho^{2}U^{{\dagger}})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Bd}}\right),

(113)

where we use Lemma
9
to derive the last equality.
Third, from the symmetry between
\Delta_{2}
and
\Delta_{3}
we have

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{3}}}\right]=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{2}}}\right]=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Bd}}\right).

(114)

Forth,

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\Delta_{4}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)(U\otimes U\otimes I\otimes I)\,\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(4)}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathrm{S}_{4}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O_{U}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)T_{\pi}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\big[(\Tr(O_{U}))^{2}+\Tr(O_{U}^{2})+4\Tr(O_{U})\Tr(O_{U}\rho)+(\Tr(O_{U}))^{2}\Tr(\rho^{2})+4\Tr(O_{U})\Tr(O_{U}\rho^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+4\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho)+\Tr(O_{U}^{2})\Tr(\rho^{2})+2(\Tr(O_{U}\rho))^{2}+4\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho^{2})+2\Tr(O_{U}\rho O_{U}\rho)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Bd}}\right),

(115)

where
O_{U}=U^{{\dagger}}OU
, and the last equality follows from Lemma
9
again.

Therefore, Eq. (
111
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\Tr\!\Big[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O^{\otimes 2}\otimes\rho^{\otimes 2}}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\otimes 2}}}\right]\Big]=\frac{2}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}+\frac{2s}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}}}\right)\mathcal{O}(dB).

(116)

By plugging Eq. (
116
) into Eq. (
110
), we have

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right)}}\right]\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\frac{\mathcal{O}(dB)}{(d+s)(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}+\frac{2s}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)dB}}\right],

(117)

which confirms the inequality (
108
).

At last, we show that the inequality (
108
) can be saturated when
\rho
is a pure state and
O=\sqrt{B/d}\,I\in\mathrm{Obs}(B)
.
In this case,

\displaystyle\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((O\otimes\rho^{\top})\hat{X}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(O\otimes\rho^{\top})(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\top})}}\right]-\frac{d+1+s}{s}\Tr(O\otimes\rho^{\top})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Var\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}\sqrt{\frac{B}{d}}\Tr(\rho\hat{\psi})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{B}{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\Big[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\rho\otimes\rho){\mathbb{E}}[\hat{\psi}\otimes\hat{\psi}]}}\right)-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Tr(\rho{\mathbb{E}}[\hat{\psi}])}}\right)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{B}{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\bigg[\Tr\bigg((\rho\otimes\rho)\frac{\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}{\kappa_{2}}\bigg)-\big(\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\rho I/\kappa_{1}}}\right)\big)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{B}{d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)}{s}}}\right]^{2}\bigg[\frac{2}{d(d+1)}-\frac{1}{d^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}}}\right)dB}}\right],

(118)

where
(a)
follows from Eq. (
4
) in the main text and the fact that
the state
\hat{\psi}
is chosen randomly from a 4-design state ensemble.
This completes the proof of Lemma
10
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
2

In preparation for proving Lemma
2
,
it will be convenient to first calculate the second moment of the snapshot
\hat{X}
, which is defined in Eq. (
9
) of the main text.

Lemma 11
.

The expectation of
\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}
(over both random state preparation and measurement outcomes) is

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]=\frac{2d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)}{s^{2}(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)-\frac{d+1+s}{s}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{5}+\tilde{\Delta}_{6}}}\right)-\frac{(d+1+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{7},

(119)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)_{1,3}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)_{2,4},

(120)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((I\otimes I\otimes U\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I_{1}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}}\right)_{2,3,4}}}\right](I\otimes I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)_{1,3}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{4}}}\right]}}\right)^{\top_{2,4}},

(121)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((U\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I_{3}\otimes\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(3)}}}\right)_{1,2,4}}}\right](U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(2)}}}\right)_{1,3}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{4}}}\right]}}\right)^{\top_{2,4}},

(122)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((U\otimes I\otimes U\otimes I)\,\Pi_{\operatorname{sym}}^{(4)}\,(U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I\otimes U^{{\dagger}}\otimes I)}}\right)^{\top_{2,4}},

(123)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{5}

\displaystyle:=\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I,\qquad\tilde{\Delta}_{6}:=I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}},\qquad\tilde{\Delta}_{7}:=I\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I,

(124)

and
\top_{2,4}
denotes the partial transpose on the second and forth subsystems.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}^{\top})-(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)}{s}}}\right)^{\otimes 2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}\otimes\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}}}\right]^{\top_{2,4}}-\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(d+1+s)}{s^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right]^{\top_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(d+1+s)}{s^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I\otimes I\otimes\hat{\phi}\otimes\hat{\psi}}}\right]^{\top_{4}}+\frac{(d+1+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{2d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)}{s^{2}(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)+\frac{(d+1+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(d+1+s)}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)+s\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}{d(d+1)(d+s)}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{d(d+1)(d+s)(d+1+s)}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[I\otimes I\otimes\frac{(d+1+s)(I\otimes I)+s\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}{d(d+1)(d+s)}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)}{s^{2}(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{d+1+s}{s}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}+\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)-\frac{(d+1+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes I\otimes I}}\right),

(125)

which confirms Lemma
11
.
Here the equality
(a)
follows from Lemma
1
in the main text,
Lemma
6
in Appendix
D
, and the
relation
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(U\otimes U^{{\dagger}})T_{(1,2)}}}\right]^{\top_{2}}=\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}
.
∎

Now we are ready to bound the covariance term

\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{k,\ell}}}\right)

for each combination of the four indices
(i,j,k,\ell)
with
i\neq j
and
k\neq\ell
.
In the rest of this appendix, Appendix
F.1
deals with the case in which exactly one index matches in different
positions
(i=\ell
or
j=k)
;
Appendix
F.2
deals with the case in which exactly one index matches in the same position
(i=k
or
j=\ell)
;
Appendix
F.3
deals with the case in which both indices match with order swapped
(i=\ell
and
j=k)
;
and Appendix
F.4
deals with the case in which both indices match with the same order
(i=k
and
j=\ell)
.
Lemmas
12
–
15
in the following subsections together will confirm Lemma
2
in the main text.

F.1
Exactly one index matches in different positions

Lemma 12
.

For all distinct
i,j,k
, we have
\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,k}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right)
, where
\wp=\Tr(\rho^{2})
.

Proof of Lemma
12
.

First, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,k}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}}}\right)^{*}}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}}}\right)^{*}}}\right]

\displaystyle\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{k}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]-\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)d\,\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right]\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)d\,\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right]^{*}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{k}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]-d^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2},

(126)

where
(a)
holds because
O\otimes\rho^{\top}
,
\hat{X}_{j}
,
\hat{X}_{k}
are Hermitian,
and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}]={\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}]={\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}]^{2}=\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}^{2}=d\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}.

(127)

The second moment term in the last line of Eq. (
126
) can be further calculated as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{k}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{i}\big]\otimes{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{k}\big]}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\big]}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]}}\right).

(128)

The expression of
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]
in Lemma
11
has 7 terms.
Plugging them into Eq. (
128
), respectively, we get

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{5}}}\right]=d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0,

(129)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{6}}}\right]=d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0,

(130)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{7}}}\right]=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0;

(131)

and

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{1}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(132)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(133)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{3}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(134)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right).

(135)

Here Eqs. (
132
)–(
135
) can be derived by using tensor-network diagrams.
For example,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[4(\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}}))^{2}+2d(\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}}))^{2}+4\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}}OU\rho U^{{\dagger}})+2d\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}}OU\rho U^{{\dagger}})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(136)

where the last equality follows from Lemma
9
.

Therefore, by Eqs. (
126
) and (
128
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\,\frac{2d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)}{s^{2}(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d^{4}}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\min\{1,B\wp\}}{\kappa_{2}}+\frac{sd\min\{1,B\wp\}}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s(s-1)d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}{2\kappa_{4}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(137)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
11
and Eqs. (
129
)–(
131
), and

(b)
follows from Eqs. (
132
)–(
135
).
∎

F.2
Exactly one index matches in the same position

Lemma 13
.

For all distinct
i,j,k
, we have
\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{k,j}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{3}}{s}\wp+d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right)
,
where
\wp=\Tr(\rho^{2})
.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

First, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{k,j}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{k}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)^{*}}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{k}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)^{*}}}\right]

\displaystyle\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}}}\right)}}\right]-\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)d\,\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right]\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)d\,\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right]^{*}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]-d^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2},

(138)

where
(a)
holds because
O\otimes\rho^{\top}
,
\hat{X}_{j}
,
\hat{X}_{k}
are Hermitian,
and
{\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}]={\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}]={\mathbb{E}}[\hat{X}]^{2}=\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}^{2}=d\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}
.
The second moment term in the last line can be further calculated as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{k}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{i}\big]\otimes I\otimes I}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\big]\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}_{k}\big]}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right).

(139)

The expression of
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]
in Lemma
11
has 7 terms.
Plugging them into Eq. (
139
), respectively, we get

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{5}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]=d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0,

(140)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{6}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]=d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0,

(141)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{7}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}\geq 0,

(142)

and

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\wp}}\right),

(143)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(144)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\wp}}\right),

(145)

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right).

(146)

Here Eqs. (
143
)–(
146
) can be derived by using tensor-network diagrams.
For example,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Omega\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{4}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Omega}}\right)}}\right]

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}+5d+6}}\right)(\Tr(OU\rho U^{{\dagger}}))^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}+5d+6}}\right)\Tr(O^{2}U\rho^{2}U^{{\dagger}})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(147)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
9
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{k,j}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\,{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\Tr\!\bigg[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\frac{2d^{2}(d+1)^{2}(d+s)}{s^{2}(d+s+1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{2}}\tilde{\Delta}_{1}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{2}+\frac{s}{\kappa_{3}}\tilde{\Delta}_{3}+\frac{s(s-1)}{2\kappa_{4}}\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\Upsilon_{\mathcal{U}}}}\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\frac{d^{4}}{s^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d\wp}{\kappa_{2}}+\frac{sd\min\{1,B\wp\}}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{sd^{2}\wp}{\kappa_{3}}+\frac{s(s-1)d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}{2\kappa_{4}}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{3}}{s}\wp+d^{2}\min\{1,B\wp\}}}\right),

(148)

where
(a)
follows from Lemma
11
and Eqs. (
140
)–(
142
),
and
(b)
follows from Eqs. (
143
)–(
146
).
∎

F.3
Both indices match with order swapped

Lemma 14
.

For distinct
i,j
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{3}}{s^{4}}+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{3}}+\frac{d\sqrt{d\wp}}{s^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{d\wp}}{s}+\wp}}\right)d^{2}B}}\right].

(149)

Proof of Lemma
14
.

For distinct
i,j
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,i}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{i}}}\right)^{*}}}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right]-d^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2},

\displaystyle\leq\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]}}\right).

(150)

Define

\displaystyle\xi(\mu,\nu):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{\mu}\,\tilde{\Delta}_{\nu}}}\right)}}\right|,\qquad\mu,\nu\in\{1,2,\dots,7\}.

(151)

Then, according to Lemma
11
, we can expand the last line of Eq. (
150
) as

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{4}}{s^{4}}\xi(1,1)+\frac{d^{3}}{s^{3}}[\xi(1,2)+\xi(1,3)+\xi(2,1)+\xi(3,1)]+\frac{d}{s}[\xi(2,4)+\xi(3,4)+\xi(4,2)+\xi(4,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}[\xi(2,2)+\xi(2,3)+\xi(1,4)+\xi(3,2)+\xi(3,3)+\xi(4,1)]+\xi(4,4)+\frac{(d+s)^{4}}{s^{4}}\xi(7,7)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d(d+s)}{s^{2}}[\xi(3,5)+\xi(3,6)+\xi(2,5)+\xi(2,6)+\xi(5,2)+\xi(5,3)+\xi(6,2)+\xi(6,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)}{s^{3}}[\xi(1,5)+\xi(1,6)+\xi(5,1)+\xi(6,1)]+\frac{(d+s)}{s}[\xi(4,5)+\xi(4,6)+\xi(5,4)+\xi(6,4)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{(d+s)^{3}}{s^{3}}[\xi(5,7)+\xi(6,7)+\xi(7,5)+\xi(7,6)]+\frac{d(d+s)^{2}}{s^{3}}[\xi(2,7)+\xi(3,7)+\xi(7,2)+\xi(7,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{(d+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}[\xi(5,5)+\xi(5,6)+\xi(6,5)+\xi(6,6)+\xi(4,7)+\xi(7,4)]+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)^{2}}{s^{4}}[\xi(1,7)+\xi(7,1)]\bigg).

(152)

\xi(\mu,\nu)
using tensor-network diagrams, as summarized in Table
4
.

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{12}\,\Big[(\Tr(O))^{2}(\Tr(\rho))^{2}+2\Tr(O)\Tr(\rho)\Tr(OU\rho U^{\dagger})+(\Tr(O))^{2}\Tr(\rho^{2})+2\Tr(O)\Tr(OU\rho^{2}U^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\Tr(O^{2})(\Tr(\rho))^{2}+2\Tr(\rho)\Tr(O^{2}U\rho U^{\dagger})+\Tr(O^{2})\Tr(\rho^{2})+2\Tr(O^{2}U\rho^{2}U^{\dagger})\Big].

(153)

This equation and Lemma
9
together imply that

\displaystyle\xi(1,3)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\,\tilde{\Delta}_{3}}}\right]}}\right|\leq\frac{dB+2\sqrt{dB}+B+2}{6}=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right).

(154)

The other bounds in Table
4
can be derived in a similar way.

Table 4:

Upper bounds for
\xi(\mu,\nu)
derived by using tensor-network diagrams.
Here
\wp=\Tr(\rho^{2})
.

\begin{array}[]{c|ccccccc}\hline\cr\hline\cr&\quad\ \ \nu=1&\quad\ \ \nu=2&\quad\ \ \nu=3&\quad\ \ \nu=4&\quad\ \ \nu=5&\quad\ \ \nu=6&\quad\ \ \nu=7\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=1&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=2&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(dB\sqrt{\wp}\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=3&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(dB\sqrt{\wp}\big)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=4&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d^{2}B\wp\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=5&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(B\wp\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=6&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(B\wp\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(d\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\mu=7&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\wp}B\big)&\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(dB}}\right)\\[2.15277pt]
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

By combining Eqs. (
150
), (
152
), and Table
4
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{j,i}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{4}}{s^{4}}dB+\frac{d^{3}}{s^{3}}dB+\frac{d}{s}dB\sqrt{d\wp}+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}dB\sqrt{d\wp}+d^{2}B\wp+\frac{(d+s)^{4}}{s^{4}}dB+\frac{d(d+s)}{s^{2}}dB\sqrt{d\wp}+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)}{s^{3}}B\sqrt{d\wp}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{(d+s)}{s}dB\sqrt{d\wp}+\frac{(d+s)^{3}}{s^{3}}B\sqrt{d\wp}+\frac{d(d+s)^{2}}{s^{3}}dB+\frac{(d+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}dB\sqrt{d\wp}+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)^{2}}{s^{4}}dB\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{3}}{s^{4}}+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{3}}+\frac{d\sqrt{d\wp}}{s^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{d\wp}}{s}+\wp}}\right)d^{2}B}}\right],

(155)

which confirms Lemma
14
.
∎

F.4
Both indices match with the same order

Lemma 15
.

For distinct
i,j
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{i,j}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{d^{4}}{s^{4}}+\frac{d^{3}}{s^{3}}+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}+\frac{d}{s}+1}}\right)d^{2}B\wp}}\right]

(156)

Proof of Lemma
15
.

For distinct
i,j
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Lambda_{i,j},\Lambda_{i,j}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{i}}}\right)}}\right]-d^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\,\mathcal{U}(\rho)}}\right)}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{i}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right],

(157)

Note that the trace inside the last line of Eq. (
157
) can be further written as

\displaystyle\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\hat{X}_{i}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\hat{X}_{i}\otimes\hat{X}_{i}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{X}_{j}\otimes\hat{X}_{j}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)T_{(1,5)(2,6)(3)(4)}}}\right).

(158)

So the covariance satisfies

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right),\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}}}\right)\hat{X}_{i}\hat{X}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes{\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathbb{E}}\big[\hat{X}\otimes\hat{X}\big]\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)T_{(1,5)(2,6)(3)(4)}}}\right].

(159)

Define

\displaystyle\zeta(\mu,\nu):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{\mu}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{\nu}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)T_{(1,5)(2,6)(3)(4)}}}\right]}}\right|\quad\mu,\nu\in\{1,2,\dots,7\}.

(160)

According to Lemma
11
, we can then expand the RHS of Eq. (
159
) as

RHS of Eq. (
159
)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{d^{4}}{s^{4}}\zeta(1,1)+\frac{d^{3}}{s^{3}}[\zeta(1,2)+\zeta(1,3)+\zeta(2,1)+\zeta(3,1)]+\frac{d}{s}[\zeta(2,4)+\zeta(3,4)+\zeta(4,2)+\zeta(4,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d^{2}}{s^{2}}[\zeta(2,2)+\zeta(2,3)+\zeta(1,4)+\zeta(3,2)+\zeta(3,3)+\zeta(4,1)]+\zeta(4,4)+\frac{(d+s)^{4}}{s^{4}}\zeta(7,7)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d(d+s)}{s^{2}}[\zeta(3,5)+\zeta(3,6)+\zeta(2,5)+\zeta(2,6)+\zeta(5,2)+\zeta(5,3)+\zeta(6,2)+\zeta(6,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)}{s^{3}}[\zeta(1,5)+\zeta(1,6)+\zeta(5,1)+\zeta(6,1)]+\frac{(d+s)}{s}[\zeta(4,5)+\zeta(4,6)+\zeta(5,4)+\zeta(6,4)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{(d+s)^{3}}{s^{3}}[\zeta(5,7)+\zeta(6,7)+\zeta(7,5)+\zeta(7,6)]+\frac{d(d+s)^{2}}{s^{3}}[\zeta(2,7)+\zeta(3,7)+\zeta(7,2)+\zeta(7,3)]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{(d+s)^{2}}{s^{2}}[\zeta(5,5)+\zeta(5,6)+\zeta(6,5)+\zeta(6,6)+\zeta(4,7)+\zeta(7,4)]+\frac{d^{2}(d+s)^{2}}{s^{4}}[\zeta(1,7)+\zeta(7,1)]\bigg).

(161)

To bound the covariance, we derive bounds for all
\zeta(\mu,\nu)
using tensor-network diagrams, as summarized in Table
5
.

\displaystyle=(C_{1}d^{2}+C_{2}d+C_{3})\Tr(O_{U}^{2})\Tr(\rho^{2})+(C_{4}d^{2}+C_{5}d+C_{6})(\Tr(O_{U}\rho))^{2}+(C_{7}d^{2}+C_{8}d+C_{9})\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\ +(C_{10}d+C_{11})\Tr(O_{U})\Tr(O_{U}\rho)+(C_{12}d+C_{13})(\Tr(O_{U}))^{2}\Tr(\rho^{2})+(C_{14}d+C_{15})\Tr(O_{U})\Tr(O_{U}\rho^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\ +(C_{16}d+C_{17})\Tr(O_{U}^{2}\rho)+(C_{18}d+C_{19})\Tr(O_{U}\rho O_{U}\rho)+(C_{20}d+C_{21})\Tr(O^{2})+(C_{22}d+C_{23})(\Tr(O))^{2},

(162)

where
C_{1},C_{2},\dots,C_{23}
are constants, and
O_{U}=U^{{\dagger}}OU
.
This equation and Lemma
9
together imply that

\displaystyle\zeta(4,3)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Tr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes O\otimes\rho^{\top}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(I\otimes I\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{4}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{\Delta}_{3}\otimes I\otimes I}}\right)T_{(1,5)(2,6)(3)(4)}}}\right]}}\right|=\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(d^{2}B\wp}}\right).

(163)

The other bounds in Table
5
can be derived in a similar way.

Upper bounds for
\zeta(\mu,\nu)
derived by using tensor-network diagrams.
Here
\wp=\Tr(\rho^{2})
.
